, which are similar to the estimated parameters often found when a two-state Markov-switching GARCH model is estimated on data. Indeed, when a two-state Markov-switching GARCH model is estimated on data, one state is often a persistent low-volatility state in which
\alpha
is relatively low and
\beta
is relatively high. The other is often a persistent high-volatility state in which
\alpha
is relatively high and
\beta
is relatively low, which, according to
Haas et al. (2004b)
, may indicate a tendency to overreact to news possibly due to a prevailing panic-like mood. The means and standard deviations of the estimated parameters are estimated from
2500
replications where a replication consists of first simulating
T
observations from the model and then estimating the model from the
T
observations; the R package MSGARCH developed by
Ardia et al. (2019)
is used for both purposes. The finite-sample properties of the MLE for the benchmark model are good. Indeed, the estimated means of the estimated parameters converge to the true parameters and the estimated standard deviations of the estimated parameters converge to zero.

Table
1
investigates both what happens when the two states of the benchmark model are more similar and what happens when the two states of the benchmark model are more different. In both cases, the finite-sample properties of the MLE are good; best, however, in the case where the two states are more different since, in this case, it is easier to determine whether an observation is from one state or the other making it easier to estimate the parameters in the two states.

Table
2
investigates what happens when the second state of the benchmark model is less persistent. Although the finite-sample properties of the MLEs in the first state are good, the finite-sample properties of the MLEs in the second state are, somewhat surprisingly, not entirely satisfactory. There is, however, a natural explanation for this. Because the second state is less persistent, it is less likely to observe a relatively long sequence of consecutive observations from the second state making it more difficult to estimate the parameters in the second state; something which is important to keep in mind when a two-state Markov-switching GARCH model is applied to data.

7
Conclusion

State space models and their extensions are ubiquitous in economics and finance, so statistical inference for them – including estimation, which is typically done by maximum likelihood estimation – is of significant practical importance.

In this paper, we proved both consistency and asymptotic normality of the MLE for a Markov-switching observation-driven model, that is, an observation-driven state space model where
X
is finite. To the best of our knowledge, these results are the first of their kind in the literature. As a special case, we also gave conditions under which the MLE for the widely applied Markov-switching GARCH model by
Haas et al. (2004b)
is both consistent and asymptotically normal. Again, this is new to the literature and extends
Kandji and Misko (2024)
, who gave conditions under which it is only consistent.

Several extensions of this paper are possible. One is to generalise the results in this paper to a higher-order Markov-switching observation-driven model, that is, a model where
X_{j,t+1}=\phi_{j}(Y_{t},...,Y_{t-q+1},X_{j,t},...,X_{j,t-p+1};\bm{\upsilon}_{j})
. Another is to generalise them to a multivariate Markov-switching observation-driven model. Both follow by using similar arguments to the ones in this paper. A final, and very interesting, extension of this paper is to generalise the results to an observation-driven state space model where
X
is not necessarily finite to cover more of the examples in the introduction. We conjecture that the results in this paper can be extended to an observation-driven state space model where
X
is compact relatively easily by combining the arguments in this paper and the ones in
Douc et al. (2004)
and
Kasahara and Shimotsu (2019)
. We, however, leave this for future research.

Appendix

The appendix is organised as follows. The main proofs, that is, the proofs of Lemmas
1
and
2
, Theorems
2
and
3
, and Proposition
1
, are collected in Section
A
. The other proofs are collected in Section
B
, and supplementary material to the other proofs is collected in Section
C
. In the following,
C\in\mathbb{R}
is an arbitrary constant that can change from line to line.

Appendix A
Main Proofs

A.1
Proof of Lemma
1

For each
j\in\{1,...,J\}
,
(X_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j}))_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a stochastic process taking values in
\mathcal{X}_{j}
given by

X_{j,t+1}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j})=\phi_{j,t}(X_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j});\bm{\upsilon}_{j})

with

\phi_{j,t}(X_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j});\bm{\upsilon}_{j})=\phi_{j}(Y_{t},X_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j});\bm{\upsilon}_{j}).

Note that

\Lambda(\phi_{j,t};\bm{\upsilon}_{j})=\sup_{\underset{x_{j}\neq y_{j}}{x_{j},y_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j}}}\frac{|\phi_{j,t}(x_{j};\bm{\upsilon}_{j})-\phi_{j,t}(y_{j};\bm{\upsilon}_{j})|}{|x_{j}-y_{j}|}\leq\sup_{x_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j}}\left|\nabla_{x_{j}}\phi_{j,t}(x_{j};\bm{\upsilon}_{j})\right|=\Lambda_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j}).

The conclusion thus follows from Theorem 3.1 in
Bougerol (1993)
since, for each
j\in\{1,...,J\}
,

(i)

there exists an
x_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j}
such that
\mathbb{E}[\log^{+}\sup_{\bm{\upsilon}_{j}\in\bm{\Upsilon}_{j}}|\phi_{j,t}(x_{j};\bm{\upsilon}_{j})-x_{j}|]<\infty
,

(ii)

\mathbb{E}[\log^{+}\sup_{\bm{\upsilon}_{j}\in\bm{\Upsilon}_{j}}\Lambda_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j})]<\infty
, and

(iii)

-\infty\leq\mathbb{E}[\log\sup_{\bm{\upsilon}_{j}\in\bm{\Upsilon}_{j}}\Lambda_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j})]<0

by assumption.

A.2
Proof of Lemma
2

The proof consists of two parts. The first part considers the stochastic process
(\bm{\pi}_{t\mid t}(\bm{\theta}))_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
, and the second part considers the stochastic process
(\hat{\bm{\pi}}_{t\mid t}(\bm{\theta}))_{t\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
.

First,
(\bm{\pi}_{t\mid t}(\bm{\theta}))_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a stochastic process taking values in
\mathcal{S}
given by

\bm{\pi}_{t\mid t}(\bm{\theta})=\bm{\phi}_{t}(\bm{\pi}_{t-1\mid t-1}(\bm{\theta});\bm{\theta})

with

\bm{\phi}_{t}(\bm{\pi}_{t-1\mid t-1}(\bm{\theta});\bm{\theta})=\mathbf{F}_{t}(\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\bm{\pi}_{t-1\mid t-1}(\bm{\theta});\bm{\theta})\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\bm{\pi}_{t-1\mid t-1}(\bm{\theta}).

Let

\Lambda(\bm{\phi}_{t};\bm{\theta})=\sup_{\underset{\mathbf{x}\neq\mathbf{y}}{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}}}\frac{||\bm{\phi}_{t}(\mathbf{x};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}(\mathbf{y};\bm{\theta})||_{2}}{||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{2}}.

The fact that
(\bm{\pi}_{t\mid t}(\bm{\theta}))_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
is stationary and ergodic for all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
follows from Proposition 1 in
Krabbe (2025)
, which is a special case of Theorem 3.1 in
Bougerol (1993)
, if

(i)

there exists an
\mathbf{s}\in\mathcal{S}
such that
\mathbb{E}[\log^{+}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}||\bm{\phi}_{t}(\mathbf{s};\bm{\theta})-\mathbf{s}||_{2}]<\infty
,

(ii)

\mathbb{E}[\log^{+}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\Lambda(\bm{\phi}_{t};\bm{\theta})]<\infty
, and

(iii)

there exists an
r\in\mathbb{N}
such that
-\infty\leq\mathbb{E}[\log\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\Lambda(\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)};\bm{\theta})]<0
.

Condition (i) is trivial. To show Conditions (ii) and (iii), note that

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=j\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{u})=\sum_{i=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=j\mid S_{t-1}=i,\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{u})\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t-1}=i\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{u})

for all
t,u\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
t\leq u
where

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=j\mid S_{t-1}=i,\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{u})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u},S_{t}=j\mid S_{t-1}=i,\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1})}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u},S_{t}=k\mid S_{t-1}=i,\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=j,S_{t-1}=i)\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=j\mid S_{t-1}=i,\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1})}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=k,S_{t-1}=i)\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=k\mid S_{t-1}=i,\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=j)\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=j\mid S_{t-1}=i)}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=k)\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=k\mid S_{t-1}=i)}.

Hence,
\pi_{j,t\mid u}(\bm{\theta}):=\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t}=j\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{u}),j\in\{1,...,J\}
is given by

\pi_{j,t\mid u}(\bm{\theta})=\sum_{i=1}^{J}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=j)p_{ij}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=k)p_{ik}}\pi_{i,t-1\mid u}(\bm{\theta})

for all
t,u\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
t\leq u
and
\bm{\pi}_{t\mid u}(\bm{\theta}):=(\pi_{1,t\mid u}(\bm{\theta}),...,\pi_{J,t\mid u}(\bm{\theta}))^{\prime}
is given by

\bm{\pi}_{t\mid u}(\bm{\theta})=\mathbf{M}_{t\mid u}^{\prime}(\bm{\theta})\bm{\pi}_{t-1\mid u}(\bm{\theta})

for all
t,u\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
t\leq u
where
\mathbf{M}_{t\mid u}(\bm{\theta})
is a row stochastic matrix, that is, a square matrix with non-negative elements where each row sum to one, with generic element

[\mathbf{M}_{t\mid u}(\bm{\theta})]_{ij}:=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=j)p_{ij}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{u}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{u}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-1},S_{t}=k)p_{ik}},\quad i,j\in\{1,...,J\}.

Thus, we have that

\bm{\pi}_{t\mid t}(\bm{\theta})=\mathbf{M}_{t\mid t}^{\prime}(\bm{\theta})\cdots\mathbf{M}_{t-r+1\mid t}^{\prime}(\bm{\theta})\bm{\pi}_{t-r\mid t}(\bm{\theta})

(A.1)

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
where

\displaystyle[\bm{\pi}_{t-r\mid t}(\bm{\theta})]_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t-r}=j\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t},S_{t-r}=j\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r})}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t},S_{t-r}=k\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r},S_{t-r}=j)\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t-r}=j\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r})}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r},S_{t-r}=k)\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(S_{t-r}=k\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r},S_{t-r}=j)[\bm{\pi}_{t-r\mid t-r}(\bm{\theta})]_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r},S_{t-r}=k)[\bm{\pi}_{t-r\mid t-r}(\bm{\theta})]_{k}},\quad j\in\{1,...,J\}.

This observation leads to Lemma
A.3
, which is proved using Lemmas
A.1
and
A.2
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\mathbf{M}
be a row stochastic matrix with generic element
m_{ij},i,j\in\{1,...,J\}
. Assume that there exist an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and a
\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{S}
such that

m_{ij}\geq\varepsilon v_{j}

for all
i,j\in\{1,...,J\}
. Then, there exists an
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that

||\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{y}||_{1}\leq\alpha||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{1}

for all
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\mathbf{M}}
be a matrix with generic element
\tilde{m}_{ij}=(1-\varepsilon)^{-1}(m_{ij}-\varepsilon v_{j}),i,j\in\{1,...,J\}
. Note that
\tilde{\mathbf{M}}
is a row stochastic matrix. Thus,

\displaystyle||\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{y}||_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{J}m_{ij}(x_{i}-y_{i})\right|

\displaystyle=(1-\varepsilon)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{J}\tilde{m}_{ij}(x_{i}-y_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varepsilon)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i=1}^{J}\tilde{m}_{ij}|x_{i}-y_{i}|

\displaystyle=(1-\varepsilon)\sum_{i=1}^{J}|x_{i}-y_{i}|=(1-\varepsilon)||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{1}

for all
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

If there exists an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
such that

p_{ij}\geq\varepsilon

for all
i,j\in\{1,...,J\}
, then, for all
\tau,t\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\tau\leq t
, there exists a
\mathbf{v}_{\tau\mid t}(\bm{\theta})\in\mathcal{S}
such that

[\mathbf{M}_{\tau\mid t}(\bm{\theta})]_{ij}\geq\varepsilon[\mathbf{v}_{\tau\mid t}(\bm{\theta})]_{j}

for all
i,j\in\{1,...,J\}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{v}_{\tau\mid t}(\bm{\theta})
be a stochastic vector, that is, a vector with non-negative elements that sum to one, with generic element

[\mathbf{v}_{\tau\mid t}(\bm{\theta})]_{j}:=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{\tau}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{\tau-1},S_{\tau}=j)}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{\tau}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{\tau-1},S_{\tau}=k)},\quad j\in\{1,...,J\}.

Then,

\displaystyle[\mathbf{M}_{\tau\mid t}(\bm{\theta})]_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{\tau}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{\tau-1},S_{\tau}=j)p_{ij}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{\tau}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{\tau-1},S_{\tau}=k)p_{ik}}

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{\tau}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{\tau-1},S_{\tau}=j)}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{\tau}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{\tau-1},S_{\tau}=k)}=\varepsilon[\mathbf{v}_{\tau\mid t}(\bm{\theta})]_{j}

for all
i,j\in\{1,...,J\}
.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Assume that there exists an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
such that

p_{ij}\geq\varepsilon

for all
i,j\in\{1,...,J\}
. Then, there exists an
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that

||\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{x};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{y};\bm{\theta})||_{2}\leq\alpha^{r}C||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{2}

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

First,
||\mathbf{z}||_{2}\leq||\mathbf{z}||_{1}
for all
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}
implies that

||\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{x};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{y};\bm{\theta})||_{2}\leq||\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{x};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{y};\bm{\theta})||_{1}

(A.2)

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
. To ease the notation, let
p_{j,t}:=\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r},S_{t-r}=j)
. Note that, by Equation (
A.1
),

\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{z};\bm{\theta})=\mathbf{M}_{t\mid t}^{\prime}(\bm{\theta})\cdots\mathbf{M}_{t-r+1\mid t}^{\prime}(\bm{\theta})\tilde{\mathbf{z}}

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}
where

\tilde{z}_{j}=\frac{p_{j,t}z_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}z_{k}}.

Thus, by Lemmas
A.1
and
A.2
, there exists an
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that

||\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{x};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{y};\bm{\theta})||_{1}\leq\alpha^{r}||\tilde{\mathbf{x}}-\tilde{\mathbf{y}}||_{1}

(A.3)

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
where

||\tilde{\mathbf{x}}-\tilde{\mathbf{y}}||_{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left|\frac{p_{j,t}x_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}-\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}y_{k}}\right|.

Note that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{p_{j,t}x_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}-\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}y_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{p_{j,t}x_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}-\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}+\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}-\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}y_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{p_{j,t}x_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}-\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}\right|+\left|\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}-\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}y_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{j,t}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}\left|x_{j}-y_{j}\right|+\frac{p_{j,t}y_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}y_{k}}\frac{1}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}(x_{k}-y_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p_{j,t}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}\left|x_{j}-y_{j}\right|+\sum_{k=1}^{J}\frac{p_{k,t}}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}p_{k,t}x_{k}}\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left|x_{j}-y_{j}\right|+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{k=1}^{J}\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|

since

p_{i,t}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\in\textup{d}\mathbf{y}_{t-r+1}^{t}\mid\mathbf{Y}_{-\infty}^{t-r},S_{t-r+1}=j)p_{ij}.

Hence, Equation (
A.3
) becomes

||\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{x};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{y};\bm{\theta})||_{1}\leq\alpha^{r}\frac{J+1}{\varepsilon}||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{1}

(A.4)

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
. Finally,
||\mathbf{z}||_{1}\leq\sqrt{J}||\mathbf{z}||_{2}
for all
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}
implies that

\alpha^{r}\frac{J+1}{\varepsilon}||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{1}\leq\alpha^{r}\frac{J+1}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{J}||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{2}

(A.5)

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
. Together, Equations (
A.2
)-(
A.5
) show that there exists an
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that

||\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{x};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}^{(r)}(\mathbf{y};\bm{\theta})||_{2}\leq\alpha^{r}\frac{J+1}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{J}||\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}||_{2}

for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{S}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

A.3
.

Condition (i) is trivial. For Condition (ii), we have that

\displaystyle|[\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{t}(\mathbf{s};\bm{\theta})-\bm{\phi}_{t}(\mathbf{s};\bm{\theta})]_{j}|

(A.6)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{J}p_{ij}s_{i}f_{j}(Y_{t};\hat{X}_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j}),\bm{\upsilon}_{j})}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};\hat{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k})}-\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{J}p_{ij}s_{i}f_{j}(Y_{t};X_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j}),\bm{\upsilon}_{j})}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};X_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k})}\right|

for all
j\in\{1,...,J\}

be given by

m_{1,t}^{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{J}p_{ij}s_{i}f_{j}(Y_{t};x_{j},\bm{\upsilon}_{j})}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};x_{k},\bm{\upsilon}_{k})}.

Then, by the mean value theorem and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, there exists an
\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon})\in\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{X}_{J}
such that

|m_{1,t}^{\bm{\theta}}(\hat{\mathbf{X}}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon}))-m_{1,t}^{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{X}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon}))|\leq||\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}m_{1,t}^{\bm{\theta}}(\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon}))||_{2}||\hat{\mathbf{X}}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon})-\mathbf{X}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon})||_{2},

where, if
m\neq j
,

\displaystyle|[\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}m_{1,t}^{\bm{\theta}}(\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon}))]_{m}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\sum_{i=1}^{J}p_{ij}s_{i}f_{j}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j}),\bm{\upsilon}_{j}))(\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lm}s_{l}|\nabla_{x_{m}}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|)}{(\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{f_{j}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j}),\bm{\upsilon}_{j})|\nabla_{x_{m}}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|}{(\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{f_{j}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{j,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{j}),\bm{\upsilon}_{j})|\nabla_{x_{m}}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|}{(\sum_{k=1}^{J}f_{k}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k}))^{2}}

and, if
m=j

\displaystyle|[\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}m_{1,t}^{\bm{\theta}}(\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{t}(\bm{\upsilon}))]_{m}|

\displaystyle+\frac{(\sum_{i=1}^{J}p_{im}s_{i}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m}))(\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lm}s_{l}|\nabla_{x_{m}}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|)}{(\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\nabla_{x_{m}}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k})}

\displaystyle+\frac{f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|\nabla_{x_{m}}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|}{(\sum_{k=1}^{J}\sum_{l=1}^{J}p_{lk}s_{l}f_{k}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|\nabla_{x_{m}}f_{m}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{m,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{m}),\bm{\upsilon}_{m})|}{\sum_{k=1}^{J}f_{k}(Y_{t};\bar{X}_{k,t}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\bm{\upsilon}_{k})}
